	\mathrm{tr}_P\nabla^2_{\mathrm{g}}K_\lambda(\hat r_a)\leq \mathrm{tr}_P\nabla^2_{\hat{\mathrm{g}}}K_\lambda(\hat r_a)+C\left(|t||\nabla^2_{\hat{\mathrm{g}}}K_\lambda(\hat r_a)|_{\hat{\mathrm g}}+ |K'_\lambda(\hat r_a)|\right).
	\]
	If $\lambda \hat r_a\leq n/2$, then $|\nabla^2_{\hat{\mathrm{g}}}K_\lambda(\hat r_a)|_{\hat{\mathrm g}}\leq C\hat r_a^{-1}|K'_\lambda(\hat r_a)|$, so that using $|t|\leq \hat r_a$
	\begin{equation}\label{eq: boundary trace}
		\begin{aligned}
			\mathrm{tr}_P\nabla^2_\mathrm{g}K_{\lambda}(\hat r_a) \leq  (-\lambda+C_0+C_1\hat r_a)|K'_{\lambda}(\hat r_a)|.
		\end{aligned}
	\end{equation}
	Choose first $ \lambda > 2C_0+1$ and then $r_0<r_*$ so small that $\lambda r_*<n/2$, and $C_1\hat r_a\leq 1$. Then $\mathrm{tr}_P\nabla^2_\mathrm{g}K_{\lambda}(\hat r_a)\leq 0$ for all $P\in G(n,n+1)$.
	
	Finally, if we define
	\[
	v(x)\coloneqq 1+\int_E K_{1}(\operatorname{dist}_{\hat{\mathrm{g}}}(x,y))\, d\mu(y),
	\]
	for the finite positive measure $\mu$ in the proof of \Cref{L: Riesz potential}, then it is easy to check that it satisfies all the required properties. Divergence of the integral at $E$ follows as in (a), and $\partial_tv=0$ on $\partial_0C_{2r_0}\setminus E$ follows from $\partial_t\hat r_a(y,0)=0$.
<!-- end proof-25 -->
<!-- proposition-7: body_md -->
\label{prop: locally finite then global stationarity}
	Let $n \geq 2$, and let $(M^{n+1}, \mathrm{g})$ be a smooth Riemannian manifold-with-boundary with (possibly empty) boundary $\partial M$. Let $\mathcal{S} \subset M$ be a relatively closed subset with locally finite $\mathcal{H}^{n-2}$ measure, i.e. $\mathcal{H}^{n - 2}(\mathcal S \cap K) < + \infty$ for every $K \Subset M$. 
	Let $V$ be an $n$-dimensional rectifiable varifold in $M$ such that 
	\[
	\delta V(X) = 0 \qquad \text{for every $X \in \mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{tan}}(M \setminus \mathcal{S})$}. 
	\]
	Then $V$ is free-boundary stationary in $M$, that is 
	\[
	\delta V(X) = 0 \qquad \text{for every $X \in \mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{tan}}(M)$}. 
	\]
	Consequently, if $\Sigma$ is a smooth proper minimal free-boundary immersion in $M \setminus \mathcal{S}$ and $|\Sigma|$ has locally finite mass, then $|\Sigma|$ is free-boundary stationary in $M$.
<!-- end proposition-7 -->
<!-- proof-26: body_md -->
Let $U\Subset M$ be a subset of the domain of a coordinate chart such that $U$ is either a metric ball or a metric half-cylinder in Fermi coordinates with respect to $\partial M$ as in \Cref{lemma: Riesz Riemannian}, and let $X\in \mathfrak X^\mathrm{tan}(U)$. The global statement then follows from this local one after covering $\operatorname{spt} X$ by finitely many sufficiently small metric balls or half-cylinders. 
	
	Choose $\operatorname{spt} X \Subset U_0 \Subset U_1\Subset U$ and set $E = \mathcal{S} \cap \overline{U}_1$, so that $E$ is compact and satisfies $\mathcal{H}^{n - 2}(E) < \infty$ by hypothesis. By \Cref{lemma: Riesz Riemannian}, there is a function $v\in C^\infty(U\setminus E)$ such that $v\geq 1$ on $\overline{U}_1\setminus E$, $v(x)\to +\infty$ as $x\to E$ and $\mathrm{tr}_P\nabla^2_\mathrm{g}v\leq 0$ for all $P\in G(n, n+1)$. In particular, $v$ is superharmonic.
	
	We now prove a logarithmic Caccioppoli estimate. Let $\eta \in C_c^\infty(U_1)$ satisfy $\eta \equiv 1$ on $U_0$, and let 
	\[
	q_\ell(v) \coloneqq \left( \frac{1}{v} - \frac{1}{\ell} \right)_+, \qquad \text{for $\ell > 1$}. 
	\]
	Extend $v$ by $+\infty$ on $E$.  Lower semicontinuity and the fact that $\operatorname{spt}\eta$ is compact imply that $\operatorname{spt}\eta\cap\{v\leq\ell\}$ is a compact subset of $U_1\setminus\mathcal S$. Thus $Y_\ell\coloneqq\eta^2q_\ell(v)\nabla^{\mathrm g}v$ is supported away from $\mathcal S$ and is tangent to $\partial M$ in the boundary case.
	Free-boundary stationarity away from $\mathcal S$ gives 
	\[
	0 = \delta V(Y_\ell) = \int_{G_n(U_1)}[\eta^2 q_\ell(v) \mathrm{tr}_P \nabla^2_\mathrm{g} v + \langle \nabla_P(\eta^2 q_\ell(v)), \nabla_P v \rangle] \, dV(x, P). 
	\]
	The first term is non-positive, thus implying 
	\[
	\int_{G_n(U_1)} \langle \nabla_P (\eta^2 q_\ell(v)), \nabla_P v\rangle  \, dV(x, P) \geq 0. 
	\]
	Now, on $\{v < \ell\}$, there holds $\nabla_P q_\ell(v) = -v^{-2} \nabla_P v$. Therefore, 
	\begin{align*}
		A_\ell \coloneqq \int_{\{v < \ell\}} \eta^2 \vert \nabla_P \log(v) \vert^2 \, dV & \leq 2 \int_{\{v < \ell\}} \eta \left( 1 - \frac{v}{\ell} \right) \langle \nabla_P \eta, \nabla_P \log(v) \rangle \, dV \\ 
		& \leq \frac{1}{2} A_\ell + 2 \int \vert \nabla_P \eta \vert^2 \, dV. 
	\end{align*}
	Rearranging, 
	\[
	A_\ell \leq 4 \int \vert \nabla_P \eta \vert^2 \, dV \leq 4 \Vert D\eta \Vert_\infty^2 \|V\|(\mathrm{spt} \eta) < + \infty.
	\]
	The last quantity is finite because $V$ has locally finite mass by assumption. Letting $\ell \rightarrow \infty$ gives 
	\[
	\int_{U_0} \vert \nabla_P \log(v) \vert^2 \, dV < + \infty. 
	\]
	We now construct the cut-off. Choose $\vartheta \in C^\infty(\mathbb{R})$ such that $0 \leq \vartheta \leq 1$ , $\vartheta \equiv 1$ on $(-\infty, 1]$ and $\vartheta \equiv 0$ on $[2, \infty)$. Define then $\chi_j \coloneqq \vartheta\left( j^{-1} \log(v)\right).$ For every fixed $j$, the function $\chi_j$ vanishes in an ambient neighbourhood of $E$, so that $\chi_j X$ is an admissible test vector field supported away from $\mathcal{S}$. Moreover, $\chi_j \rightarrow 1$ pointwise on $U_0 \setminus \mathcal{S}$. Lastly, 
	\[
	\int_{U_0} \vert \nabla_P \chi_j \vert^2 \, dV \leq \frac{\Vert \vartheta^\prime \Vert_\infty^2}{j^2} \int_{U_0} \vert \nabla_P \log(v) \vert^2 \, dV \rightarrow 0,  
	\]
	as $j \rightarrow \infty$. Consequently, from the stationarity condition
	\[
	0 = \delta V(\chi_j X) = \int \chi_j \mathrm{div}_P X \, dV + \int \langle \nabla_P \chi_j, X \rangle \, dV,
	\]
	we can use dominated convergence to pass the first term on the right-hand side to the limit, while for the second term we have 
	\[
	\left\vert \int \langle \nabla_P \chi_j, X \rangle \, dV \right\vert \leq \Vert X \Vert_\infty \|V\|(\mathrm{spt} X)^{1/2} \left(\int_{\mathrm{spt} X} \vert \nabla_P \chi_j \vert^2 \, dV \right)^{1/2} \rightarrow 0, 
	\]
	as $j \rightarrow \infty$. Thus, $V$ satisfies $\delta V(X) = 0$ in $U$ meaning that it is free-boundary stationary across $\mathcal{S}$.
<!-- end proof-26 -->
<!-- corollary-3: body_md -->
\label{cor: cap}
	Let $\mathcal{S}$ be as in \Cref{def: sing min hyp}. Then for every compact $K\subset M$ there exists a sequence of smooth cutoff functions $\{\chi_j\}_j$ on $M$, each supported away from $\mathcal{S}$, such that $0\leq \chi_j\leq 1$, $\chi_j\to 1$ on $\Sigma\cap K$, and $\int_{\Sigma\cap K}|\nabla_\Sigma\chi_j|^2\to 0.$ This will sometimes be denoted by $\operatorname{cap}_2(\mathcal{S}) = 0$ and referred to as having vanishing relative $2$-capacity in $\Sigma$ (see below).
<!-- end corollary-3 -->
<!-- remark-30: body_md -->
A weaker hypothesis suffices for extending stationarity when a local area-growth bound is already available. Namely, assume $\mathcal H^{n-1}(\mathcal S)=0$ and $\mathcal{H}^n(\Sigma\cap B_r(c))\leq Cr^n$ for $x\in\mathcal S$ and all sufficiently small radii for some constant $C$ uniform on compact sets. Then stationarity away from $\mathcal S$ extends across $\mathcal S$.
	
	                                                                                              
	Indeed, fix $X\in \mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{tan}}(U)$ and choose a compact $K\Subset U$ whose relative interior contains $\operatorname{spt}X$. Since $\mathcal H^{n-1}(\mathcal{S}\cap K)=0$, for every $\varepsilon>0$ we may cover $\mathcal{S} \cap K$ by finitely many balls $B_{r_i}(x_i)$, with $r_i<\varepsilon$ and $\sum_i r_i^{n-1}<\varepsilon$. Choose $0\leq\eta\leq1$, equal to $0$ near $\bigcup_iB_{r_i}(x_i)$ and to $1$ outside $\bigcup_iB_{2r_i}(x_i)$, with $|D\eta|\leq C\sum_i r_i^{-1}\mathbf 1_{B_{2r_i}(x_i)}$. The Euclidean growth bound $\|V\|(B_r(x))\leq Cr^n$ at $x\in \mathcal S$ gives
	\[
	\int |\nabla_P\eta|\,dV\leq C\sum_i r_i^{n-1}\leq C\varepsilon,
	\qquad
	\|V\|(\{\eta\neq1\})\leq C\sum_i r_i^n\leq C\varepsilon^2.
	\]
	Since $\eta X$ is supported away from $\mathcal{S}$, $0=\delta V(\eta X)$; hence
	\[
	|\delta V(X)| \leq \|DX\|_\infty\|V\|(\{\eta\neq1\}) +\|X\|_\infty\int|\nabla_P\eta|\,dV \leq C\bigl(\|DX\|_\infty\varepsilon^2+\|X\|_\infty\varepsilon\bigr).
	\]
	Letting $\varepsilon\downarrow0$ proves that $\delta V(X)=0$.
<!-- end remark-30 -->
<!-- remark-31: body_md -->
Note that if $\Gamma$ is a boundary component of $M$, then the varifold $|\Gamma|$ is free-boundary stationary, independently of whether the mean curvature of $\Gamma$ in $M$ vanishes, even though $\Gamma$ is a smooth hypersurface. On the other hand, notice that $\Gamma$ would not be a free-boundary minimal hypersurface according to our \Cref{def: fb smooth immersed min hyp}. Therefore, free-boundary stationary varifolds are strictly more general than free-boundary minimal hypersurfaces, even if we restrict to smooth objects.
<!-- end remark-31 -->
<!-- lemma-20: body_md -->
\label{L:reflection stationary}
Let $v \in \mathbb{R}^{n+1}$ be a unit vector and let  $P^+$ be the closed half-space $\{u: u \cdot v \geq 0\}$. Let $\theta: \mathbb{R}^{n+1} \to \mathbb{R}^{n+1}$ be the reflection map across $T=v^\perp$ given by $\theta(u) = u - 2 (u \cdot v) v$. If $V$ is a free-boundary stationary integral varifold in $P^+$, then the doubled varifold 
\begin{equation*}
	\overline{V} \coloneqq  V + \theta_\sharp V 
\end{equation*}
is a stationary integral varifold in $\mathbb{R}^{n+1}$ and it is invariant under $\theta$, i.e. $\theta_\sharp \overline{V} = \overline{V}$.
<!-- end lemma-20 -->
<!-- definition-7: body_md -->
Let $V$ be a free-boundary stationary integral varifold in $M$. We define the \emph{regular set} $\operatorname{reg}(V)$ of $V$ to be
\begin{equation*}
	\operatorname{reg}(V)\coloneqq\bigg\{x \in \operatorname{spt} \|V\|:
	\begin{array}{l}
		\textrm{there exists $r>0$ such that $\operatorname{spt}\|V\|\cap B_{r}(x)$ is a smooth }
		\\
		\textrm{embedded hypersurface-with-(possibly empty) boundary in $\partial M$}
	\end{array}\bigg\}.
\end{equation*}

Similarly, we define the \emph{singular set} $\operatorname{sing}(V)$ of $V$ by
\begin{equation}
    \operatorname{sing}(V)=\operatorname{spt} \|V\|\setminus\operatorname{reg}(V).
\end{equation}
<!-- end definition-7 -->
<!-- definition-8: body_md -->
\label{D:spine}
Let $\mathbf{C}$ be an $n$-dimensional cone in $\mathbb{R}^{n+1}$. We define the \emph{spine} $S(\mathbf{C})$ of $\mathbf{C}$ to be the translation invariance set of $\mathbf{C}$, namely
\begin{equation}
    S(\mathbf{C})\coloneqq  \{v\in \mathbb{R}^{n+1}:(\eta_{v,1})_\sharp\mathbf{C}=\mathbf{C}\},
\end{equation}
which is a vector subspace of $\mathbb{R}^{n+1}$.
<!-- end definition-8 -->
<!-- definition-9: body_md -->
For $j\in\{0, 1,\dots, n\}$, we define the \textit{$j$-stratum} $\mathscr{S}_j$ as follows:
\begin{equation}
    \mathscr{S}_j\coloneqq \{x\in\operatorname{sing}(V):\mathrm{dim}(S(\widehat{\mathbf{C}}))\leq j\text{ for all } \mathbf{C}\in\operatorname{VarTan}(V,x)\}.
\end{equation}
<!-- end definition-9 -->
<!-- remark-32: body_md -->
Clearly, 
\begin{equation}
    \mathscr{S}_0\subset\mathscr{S}_1\subset\dots\subset\mathscr{S}_{n}=\operatorname{sing}(V).
\end{equation}
<!-- end remark-32 -->
<!-- proposition-8: body_md -->
\label{prop: stratification}
    Let $V$ be a free-boundary stationary integral varifold in $M$. Then, for all $j\in\{0,1,\dots, n\}$
    \begin{equation}
        \dim_\mathcal{H}\mathscr{S}_j\leq j,
    \end{equation}
    where $\dim_\mathcal{H}$ denotes the Hausdorff dimension.
<!-- end proposition-8 -->
<!-- lemma-21: body_md -->
If $(M, \mathrm g)$ is a compact connected Riemannian manifold-with-boundary with $\partial M \neq \emptyset$ such that $\operatorname{Ric}_g\geq0$ and $\partial M$ is strictly mean-convex (namely $H_{\partial M}>0$), and the free boundary stationary integral varifold $V$ in $M$ is induced by a non-empty singular properly immersed free-boundary minimal hypersurface $\Sigma$, then $\partial \Sigma \neq \emptyset$.
<!-- end lemma-21 -->
<!-- proof-27: body_md -->
We shall argue by contradiction. Notice that $\overline{\Sigma}=\operatorname{spt} \|V\|$.
By compactness of $M$, there is $c>0$ such that $H_{\partial M}\geq c$, so that, by \cite[Lemmas 2.1--2.2]{ChoeFraser}, the distance function $d_{\partial M}$ from $\partial M$ satisfies $\Delta_\mathrm{g}d_{\partial M}\leq -c$ in the barrier sense\footnote{This is stated clearly in Lemma 2.1 of \cite{ChoeFraser} for the region where $d_{\partial M}$ is smooth, and it follows from the proof of Lemma 2.2 (in the barrier sense) outside this region. This statement also appears (without proof) as Proposition 5 in \cite{XWang21}. A similar barrier construction appears in \cite[Proof of Theorem 1.1]{Perales}.}. Now suppose $x\in \overline{\Sigma}$ is such that $d_{\partial M}(x)=\operatorname{dist}_\mathrm{g}(\overline{\Sigma}, \partial M)>0$. For $\varepsilon\in(0, c/2)$, the construction in the proof of  \cite[Lemma 2.2]{ChoeFraser}, applied to the strictly mean-convex hypersurface $\partial M$, gives a neighbourhood $U$ of $x$ in $M$ and a smooth upper barrier function $u:U\to \mathbb{R}$ for $d_{\partial M}$ such that $|\nabla^\mathrm{g} u| = 1$ on $U$ and $\Delta_\mathrm gu(x)\leq -c+\varepsilon\leq -c/2<0$.\\
For $y\in U\cap \overline\Sigma$, we have
    \[
        u(x)= d_{\partial M}(x)\leq d_{\partial M}(y)\leq u(y)
    \]
since $u$ is an upper barrier for $d_{\partial M}$ and $x$ minimises distance to $\partial M$. Therefore, $\overline{\Sigma}\cap U$ lies on one side of $S=\{u=u(x)\}$ and it touches $S$ at $x$. Since $|\nabla_\mathrm{g}u|=1$, it is easy to check that $H_S(x)=-\Delta_\mathrm{g}u(x)\geq c/2>0$, and the domain $\{u\geq u(x)\}\supset \overline{\Sigma}\cap U$ is strongly $n$-convex at $x$. The contradiction then follows from White's varifold maximum principle \cite{WhiteMaxPrinciple}.
<!-- end proof-27 -->
